    Release notes

    Unreleased (4.4.0) — capacity margin, units of measure, start-up inputs, period-total constraints, FT2 import and many fixes (schema v79)

    Input databases move to data version 79. The FlexTool window, the Spine Toolbox workflow and flextool-run --migrate migrate them; upgrading prints a notice for each behaviour change that affects the database. FlexTool now needs polar-high 3.12.4 or newer (installed with FlexTool).

    Behaviour changes — read before upgrading

    Each line says what changes for a model that ran before and what to do; the details are in the sections below.

    • Capacity margin rewritten. Units count at capacity derated by availability (VRE by its profile), storage-fed units, connections and consumption by their flow, exports add to the requirement; an investment solve's shortfall and penalty are now reported in an investment → dispatch chain. Check the margin in the new capacity_margin__d.
    • discount_rate 0 now means 0 % (before: 5 %), a negative rate is an error, and an entity that invests or retires with a cost needs a lifetime. Set 0.05 to keep the old results.
    • A zero or missing invest_cost no longer blocks investment (nor a missing salvage_value retirement) where invest_method allows it. To forbid a period, use a _period method with invest_max_period 0, or not_allowed.
    • A solve-level new_stepduration combines every time series by rule (mean, harmonic mean for efficiency, sum for inflow and energy_margin_adder); before, most took the first time step's value. To keep the old energy_margin_adder demand, divide it by the number of time steps a solve step combines.
    • VRE curtailment, VRE potential and a flowGroup's cumulative_flow per period are MWh per year like the flows (a 2-day period: 182.5 times larger). Update scripts that read these tables.
    • A period-map limit that leaves out a period of the solve is an error (before: a CO2 cap of 0, no inertia requirement, no investment limit, ...). Fill in the missing periods.
    • A mistyped period key is an error (y2020 for p2020, numeric or time-step keys on a period parameter; before, the value was dropped). Correct the key; data for extra years goes in model.periods_available.
    • A profile link without profile data is an error (upper_limit or fixed; before, it held the flow at 0). Give the profile a value in the scenario's alternatives, or remove the link.
    • More input errors stop the run: out-of-range efficiency, min_load, availability, capacity_max_coeff, lifetime, ramp_cost, a minimum above its maximum, existing as a time series, a used timeline without timestep_duration, a time series with no time step on a timeline, a timeset block that starts off its timeline, a CO2 method without its value, a realized_invest_periods outside invest_periods, partial or ungrouped stochastic inflow / reservation, a time-only energy_margin_adder, capacity-margin groups across Benders regions or with a balance_within_period node (see "Input checks" and the entries below). Each error names what to fix.
    • New warnings name data that ran silently: nodes with nothing to balance them, untied zero conversion_flow_coeff flows, negative existing, time-series keys off the timeline, a CO2 cap on a method without one, an invest_method on a non-storage node, percent-like values. The model is unchanged; check them.
    • Profile and time maps without index_name are read as written. A profile keyed by periods, or a period × time profile with an unnamed level, read 0 before; a node penalty, availability or other_operational_cost time map without index_name no longer adds its time steps to the periods. Results of such models change; name the map's levels where the run says so.
    • A reserve on a group without nodes is dropped with a warning (before, it crashed the run or was met only by shortfall). Add the group's nodes if the reserve is meant.
    • An explicit 0 in a flowGroup limit (max_ / min_cumulative_flow, max_ / min_instant_flow) is now a limit of 0; before, it meant no limit. Delete the value if no limit is meant.
    • ramp_cost is now charged on unit flows with ramp_method ramp_cost or both (new cost category ramps).
    • The engine refuses a database that is not at the current version. Migrate it with flextool-run --migrate or flextool-migrate-database; the FlexTool window, the Spine Toolbox workflow and execute_flextool_workflow.py migrate first.
    • Per-period result tables of a rolling solve name the solve, not the roll, and the capacity tables the solve that realized the investments. Update scripts that filter on roll names.
    • Excel sheet names and layout changed: long names keep their __d / __dt suffix, several shipped sheets are renamed, a unit of measure header row or a unit block is added, and dispatch data has a period column. Update Power Query and scripts (CSV names do not change).
    • Smaller output changes: a storage node's entity_annuity_d_p is per MWh (was per MW; values unchanged), no_reserve groups no longer have an empty reserve-shortfall column, and only groups whose co2_method caps emissions are listed as CO2-limited.
    • FlexTool 2 import corrected (invested capacity becomes existing, CHP output2, must-run profiles, storage start / finish, availability, the kW/kWh ratio, interest 0). Re-convert FlexTool 2 workbooks.
    • Storage in stochastic solves follows each path. bind_within_solve closes the cycle on every forecast branch (before: only the one listed last), and forecasts opening in the solve's first period start from one level. The upgrade marks such databases and the run names the nodes; check their results.
    • Forecasts given at the fan's analysis time are read in later branch periods (before, they read 0 there, so a wind unit could not run). Results of multi-period stochastic models with such forecasts change.
    • A roll of a rolling stochastic solve reads only its own forecast, never a later roll's; a look-ahead time step that only a later forecast covers is an error. Extend each roll-start forecast to the end of the roll's horizon.
    • --solver-time-limit and --solver-mip-gap now reach Gurobi, CPLEX, Xpress and COPT and win over the database and option file; the environment variables are now FLEXTOOL_SOLVER_TIME_LIMIT / _MIP_GAP (the FLEXTOOL_HIGHS_* names still work for HiGHS only). Commercial runs with these flags can give other results.
    • At its time limit a MIP with a solution keeps it, with a warning; an LP or a solve without a solution stops the run, for every solver. A commercial solve stopped by another limit, or not certified optimal, now stops the run.
    • Solver option files come from one solver_config folder per run (--solver-config-dir, next to the database, the workspace, ...), not from the folder FlexTool starts in. Check the Solver config: line of the run log and move your .opt files there.
    • Other commercial-solver fixes that can change results: CPLEX now gets solver_mip_gap; one value per solver parameter, whatever its case; Xpress refuses a MIP (it returned the LP relaxation); Gurobi file-writing parameters are not passed on.
    • The memory-saving solve (--save-memory, or warm start off) could put values on the wrong variables; results of such runs can change. FlexTool now needs polar-high 3.12.4 or newer (installed with FlexTool).
    • Fixes that change results: a user constraint without capacity terms got a right-hand side of 0 when another constraint had one (since 4.0); the reported fuel, CO2 and cost of a unit with a profile and an input were too low; the reported reserve penalty could be doubled; the period node-group indicators and VRE share are now ratios of the period energies; the summary_solve.csv slack lines and the period reserve averages use the time-step weights; without realized_invest_periods a solve realizes only the realized_periods that are also invest_periods; invest_period_total could retire and retire_period_total invest; a virtual_unitsize ≤ 0 is now ignored. Details below.
    • Security: solver options are checked before a solve. Since 4.0, a database's solver_arguments (or an option file from another person's project) could add commands to a CPLEX, Xpress or COPT run. FlexTool now refuses, for every solver including HiGHS, an option name or value with a line break, control character, space, quote, ;, # or =; names are letters, digits, _ and . (CPLEX: words separated by single spaces). The error names the solve and parameter, or the option file and line. Numbers (-1, 1e-4, inf), single words and paths without spaces still pass; a value with a space, such as a file path, or ending in a backslash is now refused: use a path without spaces.
    • Solver options in the input database take no file paths or server settings. Since 4.0, a database's solver_arguments could make the solver, HiGHS included, write over or read files anywhere on your computer (for example HiGHS solution_file / log_file, Gurobi LogFile / InputFile, CPLEX workdir, Xpress TUNEROUTPUTPATH), or send the model to another computer. An option there that names a file or folder now takes only a plain file name, written in the solve's work folder (<work folder>/solve_data/subprocess/); a full path, and a Gurobi Compute Server, Cloud, WLS, token-server or licence option, is refused with a message naming the option, its value and the solve. Move such options to solver_config/<solver>.opt: the option files, flags and environment variables keep full paths and server options. Names are matched as the solver reads them (Gurobi ignores _, Xpress an XPRS_ prefix, CPLEX takes abbreviations); the full list is in the solver docs. Gurobi's ResultFile is FlexTool's own and is ignored, with a warning, wherever it is set. FlexTool's results are in output_parquet/<scenario>/ as before; a commercial solver's log is saved as <work folder>/solve_data/subprocess/<solve>.log; HiGHS' log is in the run's log, output_parquet/<scenario>/_run.log (with flextool-run or the FlexTool window).
    • solver_precommand is not run, and a value now gives a warning. FlexTool 3 ran it in front of CPLEX (for example to reserve a floating licence); FlexTool 4 does not run commands stored in a database, for safety: a database from someone else could otherwise run any program on your computer. The parameter is kept only so that older databases still read; the warning comes once per solve. To hold a floating licence, use your solver's own licence server setup; a solver wrapper set on your computer is planned for a later release.

    New features

    Units of measure and result files

    • Units of measure for nodes (node.unit_of_measure). A node can name the unit of its amounts (m3 for water, t for hydrogen, MWh_th for heat; default MWh). It is a label only: FlexTool converts nothing and no number changes. The results of the node follow it in the parquet footer, the Excel workbook (unit row, unit block, Units and _output_metadata sheets), units.csv, datapackage.json, the results database and the plots: flows and the capacity of a unit on its side per hour (m3/h, MW_th), storage and inflow in the unit (m3), annual results per year (m3/a), prices per the unit (CUR/m3). A unit whose outputs are in different units shows how its capacity is used (MW + MW_th shared, MW; MW_th per output). One quantity summed over nodes of different units, such as a nodeGroup's flows or a connection's net flow between an electricity and a water node, is labelled Mixed units of measure!; reserves, inertia, the non-synchronous share and the capacity margin stay in MW. Tables whose rows have different units get a per-row unit of measure column in Excel, a row column in units.csv and row units in the parquet footer, written only when some node is not MWh, so outputs of models whose nodes are all MWh are unchanged (except the annuity label below). The plot labels of default_plots.yaml are unit templates ({A}/h, {R}, CUR/{kR}) that read as before for MWh nodes; a label in your own plot_settings.yaml without tokens is shown as written. The dispatch plots' y-axis and the GUI hover follow the node, and the AI assistant shows the units of the inputs and results of such a node (inflow in m3, penalty_up in CUR/m3, startup_energy in m3 per MW started) and does not add up values of nodes of different units. A node without unit_of_measure is MWh, so the assistant now states every unit plainly instead of adding "(default convention)" to it. Warnings when the input data is read: a connection, commodity, nodeGroup, flowGroup or group capacity limit over nodes of different units, a reserve / inertia / non-synchronous / capacity-margin group with a member that is not MWh, and a value that looks like a rate (m3/h). Costs entered per kW or kWh (invest_cost, fixed_cost, salvage_value) are per 0.001 of the node's rate or amount: 500 CUR per m3/h of capacity is invest_cost 0.5. See Units of measure and Unit of measure.
    • Units of measure in the result workbooks and CSV folder; layout change of the Excel sheets. The scenario workbook and the scenario-comparison workbook (which had no units at all) open on a new Units sheet: one row per result sheet with its table, unit(s), semantics and description, the sheet names linking to the sheets; _output_metadata follows it. A sheet with several header rows (two or more column levels; a comparison adds the scenario level, so this is every comparison sheet except those of entity-less tables such as costs_discounted) gains one header row, unit of measure, as its last header row: a Power Query or script that reads such a sheet with a fixed number of header rows shifts by one. A sheet with one header row (in a comparison: an entity-less table, with the scenarios as columns) keeps its header at A1, but its data is now an Excel Table tbl_<sheet>, its row index values are repeated on every row instead of merged, a set-membership sheet no longer has the 0..n row-number column, and a unit block sits to the right of the Table (pd.read_excel of the whole sheet also returns those columns: read the Table, or pass usecols). The CSV files are unchanged; a new units.csv lists the unit of every value column (file, position, header labels, unit, semantics), and datapackage.json now names each field by the header written to the file (labels joined with | as Frictionless joins them, headerRows for several header rows, commentRows for header rows that are blank in every column), so every file validates with Frictionless. The header cells no longer carry comments (LibreOffice could not save a workbook with them); the Units and _output_metadata sheets hold the same information, and in a comparison _output_metadata gives the scenario of a column in its own column. A mixed-unit table with unitless columns lists per unit among its units in the results database, the plot labels and the GUI hover as on the Units sheet (for example MWh/a; per unit). units.csv and datapackage.json are reserved names in the rename section. Fixed: the per-column units of reserve__upDown__group__d (and of any mixed-unit table with several column levels) were missing from the parquet metadata, the _output_metadata sheet and datapackage.json, and the result tools of the AI assistant gave the capacity-margin penalty_cost and capacity_price columns the unit MW. A single-result write updates only its own entries in units.csv and datapackage.json. See Units in the files.
    • New result table period_share_of_year__d. For each realised period, the share of a year it covers (its hours / 8760 in the solve that realises it): the annualised per-period (…__d) tables are the period's sums divided by this share (a 48-hour period has 0.005479, so its per-period values are 182.5 times its 48-hour sums). Results saved before this version have no such table. See Costs.

    Capacity margin and reserves

    • capacity_margin_method share_of_peak with group.capacity_margin_share. The margin is the share times the group's peak demand, taken over the period's full time series (before representative periods and new_stepduration), so a sample that misses the peak still gets the real peak's margin. Periods that use different parts of one timeline each take the peak of their own part.
    • The capacity margin of each investment solve, explained. capacity_margin__d (results database group.capacity_margin) has, per solve, group and investment period, the binding time step (the largest deficit), the margin, the demand, the requirement, what firm and VRE units, storage-backed units, other units, connections and consumption count there, the shortfall, its penalty and the shadow price of the margin. capacity_margin_entity__d (group.capacity_margin_contribution) has what each unit, connection and node counts. An investment solve keeps its rows under its own name (look-ahead and forecast branch periods included), and the run log prints capacity margin shortfall: solve …, group …, period …: … MW at … (penalty … M CUR) after each solve that leaves a shortfall. The results database has both tables without the binding step (it is in the CSV and parquet tables).
    • The reserve requirement of each reserve group (#234). reserve__upDown__group__dt (results database group__reserve__upDown.requirement_t) has, per reserve, direction, group and time step, the requirement of each method the group has (timeseries, dynamic, large failure: the largest failure), the requirement that binds (the largest deficit), the reserve provided (× reliability), the reserve that requirement counts (a large-failure requirement does not count the failing unit's own reserve, so the shortfall is requirement − counted) and the shortfall. reserve__upDown__group__d (group__reserve__upDown.requirement) has the period's time-step-weighted averages of the requirement, the provision, the counted reserve and the shortfall and their step maxima; reserve__group__provider__dt (CSV and Excel only) what each unit and connection counts for each group. The requirements are the model's own constraints evaluated at the solution; the run log prints reserve requirement check: … for a step where they disagree with the solver's shortfall (at most 10 lines per solve, then a count).
    • A capacity-margin, inertia or non-synchronous group across Benders regions is an error. Each region subproblem required the whole requirement before; use one group per region.
    • A balance_within_period node in a capacity-margin group is an error (it balances per period, the margin holds per time step); a storage node with demand in a capacity-margin group gets a warning (its demand is left out of the margin).

    Model

    • Start-up fuel and start-up heat (unit__inputNode.startup_input_method, startup_energy). An input arc can also (startup_and_input) or only (startup_only) supply the energy a unit's start-ups take: startup_energy [MWh per MW started, constant or period] × MW started, taken from the node in the step the unit starts in (an event, like startup_cost). The start-up input can be fuel (a coal unit started on oil), heat (a CSP turbine warmed up from its tank) or electricity. It is priced as the node's commodity (also on a price ladder), counts in the CO2 price and caps (also when it is a group's only emitter) and in their rolling carry, and needs online variables (linear start-ups keep the model an LP). A startup_only arc has no flow: it takes no part in the conversion, capacity, input shares or ramps, so a coal unit with an oil start-up arc stays a one-input unit. Not part of constraint_flow_coeff, flow groups or the capacity margin; left out with the start-ups under reduce_unit_detail_method online / online_ramps, and a use of the online variables under integers. New outputs unit_startup_energy__dt / __d (results database unit__node startup_energy_t / startup_energy_annualized); the unit's input from the node, the commodity cost, the CO2 and the node balance include it. Input checks for the combinations that have no effect or no meaning. Default just_input: the model is unchanged.
    • User constraints summed over a period (constraint.constraint_period_method). per_timestep (default) keeps the rows of before. period_total writes one row per period over the period's total: each step's flow and online terms are multiplied by its duration and timestep weight and divided by the period's share of a year, so flows count in MWh per year and units online in unit-hours per year, and the constant is per year. Capacity terms and their existing capacity are multiplied by the hours of the year, so a flow − 0.3 × capacity row caps the annual capacity factor at 30 %. Shares such as "gas backup at most 15 % of the CSP output over the year" can now be written. A state coefficient on such a constraint is an error. A rolling solve whose rolls do not each realize whole periods leaves the constraint out with a warning; the run stops when no solve enforces it. Each forecast branch period has its own row.
    • Units online in user constraints (constraint_online_coeff). A map on unit and connection (index: constraint name) puts the number of units online in each time step on the left side of a user constraint, for example "at least two of three units online": constraint_online_coeff 1 on each unit and constant 2 with sense greater_than. One unit is virtual_unitsize (else the largest existing capacity, else 1000 MW), so the same row works for three entities or for one entity of three units. The count is a whole number with binary startup and continuous with linear; for MW online use the unit size as the coefficient. A constraint with an online term is written once per time step. The unit or connection needs online variables (startup_method linear or binary; on a unit also a minimum_time_method); a coefficient on one without them stops the run with an error naming it and the constraint. Databases are migrated automatically; nothing else changes.
    • ramp_cost is charged. A unit flow with ramp_method ramp_cost or both pays ramp_cost [CUR/MW] for every MW its step-average flow changes from the previous time step, up and down alike. A change is an event, like a start-up, so the step length does not enter: ramping 0 → X → 0 costs 2 × ramp_cost × X. The previous step is the one the ramp limits use: no cost at the first step of a solve, after a gap in the timeline or at the start of a representative period, and a rolling window pays once for the change from the flow the previous window realized. ramp_cost works without any ramp_speed; both adds the limit. On a constant_efficiency unit with one input and one output, an input-side cost prices the change of the unit's output flow. A negative ramp_cost is an error. The cost is reported in the new cost category ramps (a zero column in the cost tables of models without a ramp cost) and in the per-unit cost tables. Databases are migrated automatically (new descriptions of ramp_method and ramp_cost only); a model whose ramp_method was already ramp_cost or both with a ramp_cost value now pays that cost.

    • inertia_limit and non_synchronous_limit per time step. Both group limits now take a constant, a period map, a time map or a period+time map (before: constant or period), e.g. a non-synchronous share that steps up by hour or season, or an inertia requirement that is higher in some hours. A time map (or the time level of a period+time map) must give a value for every time step of the solve, look-ahead included: a missing time step stops the run before its first solve (also in a rolling solve) with an error naming the group, the parameter and the missing time steps (a solve whose reduce_system_detail_method leaves the limit out is not checked). An authored 0 is no inertia requirement and a non-synchronous limit of 0. Over a coarse time step (group new_stepduration) the averaged row uses the step mean of the limit (the check is on the original time steps), and the coarse-flow info line names a limit that varies within a coarse step. The hourly inertia shortfall table shows 0 instead of an empty cell in a time step without a requirement. The Excel export puts both limits also on the time sheets. Databases are migrated automatically; constant and period values are unchanged and give the same model.

    • Representative periods in stochastic solves (#125). A stochastic solve can now use a timeset with representative_period_weights (before: an error). Each forecast branch period uses the representative periods of the period it branches from, and the *_blended_weights storage methods work per branch: a branch's first period continues from the last base period of the trunk period (the shared period before the split), a later one from the previous period of the same branch; bind_within_solve_blended_weights closes once per path (every branch ends at the level the trunk period starts from); bind_within_period_blended_weights closes each branch period on itself. When the forecasts open in the solve's first period they start from the same level (bind_forward_only_blended_weights gets the new row rp_inter_period_fan_start, also with fix_start). For a storage node in an include_stochastics group the start levels of the representative blocks are tied across the forecasts inside the non-anticipativity window (non_anticipativity_rp_block_start). Two identical forecasts give the deterministic result. Databases are migrated automatically (new descriptions of storage_binding_method and representative_period_weights only); models that ran before are unchanged.

    Faster solves: leaving out detail

    • A solve can leave out unit detail (reduce_unit_detail_method). Set it on a solve to make that solve faster, typically an investment solve followed by a full dispatch. none (default) keeps everything. integers: units with startup_method binary get continuous units online and exact connections behave as regular, so the solve is an LP. integers_ramps also leaves out ramp limits and ramp costs, integers_min_limits min_load, the capacity_min_coeff floors and minimum up / down times, integers_ramps_min_limits both. online leaves out the online variables and with them the min limits and start-up costs; min_load_efficiency units use their full-load efficiency (the same fuel at full load, less at part load), ramp limits apply to the capacity, and a user constraint with a constraint_online_coeff term is left out of the solve, with a warning naming it. online_ramps is online without ramps. Under the integers levels a unit or connection whose online variables nothing in the solve still uses (no start-up cost, min load or capacity_min_coeff floor, minimum up / down time, part-load curve, online term in a user constraint, reserve, inertia, ramp limit or parent commitment left) loses them, as with startup_method no_startup: the same result from a smaller solve. The run log names these units and connections, and their online cells of that solve are empty. A level never adds detail. Every roll of a rolling solve uses the solve's level, and a realization solve uses its parent solve's levels, whatever is set on the realization solve itself (a warning names a different value set on a declared realization solve). Databases are migrated automatically; models that do not set the parameters are unchanged.
    • A solve can leave out system detail (reduce_system_detail_method). reserves leaves out the reserve requirements (and with them the units' reserve limits and the reserve ties of a stochastic solve), inertia the inertia limits, non_synchronous the non-synchronous share limits, and dc_power_flow the angles of dc_power_flow_with_angles groups: their lines become lossless transport limited by their capacity, so loop flows disappear. inertia_non_synchronous, reserves_inertia_non_synchronous and reserves_inertia_non_synchronous_dc_power_flow combine them. The capacity margin is always kept. An investment solve without reserves invests less in flexible capacity. With either parameter set, the result tables of earlier full solves keep their online, reserve, inertia and DC columns when a later solve leaves them out, a dispatch solve that continues the ramp of a solve without online variables starts its commitment and ramp cold (an info line) instead of stopping, and a Benders decomposition can split connections whose reserve, online variables or exact method the solve leaves out (its warning about integer variables now points to integers).
    • The run log and the results say what a solve left out. A solve with reduce_unit_detail_method or reduce_system_detail_method other than none writes one info line to the run log at its first roll: what each level left out, for how many units, connections and groups, and how it changes the detail that was kept (for example "the reserves of 1 unit (g1) now come from built capacity, not online capacity"); a level with nothing to leave out says so. Every roll's entry in _run_status.json records reduced (the two levels and that line). In the result tables, the cells of a feature that a solve left out are empty, not 0, in the time steps that solve realized: units online and start-ups, reserves, reserve prices and shortfall, group inertia, its largest flow and shortfall, the non-synchronous shortfall and the DC angles. A per-period cell is empty when every solve that realized the period left the feature out. Costs are unchanged. Models that do not set the parameters get the same results as before.

    Databases and projects

    • flextool-run --migrate (also run_flextool.py, python -m flextool.cli.cmd_run_flextool) migrates an older input database before the run, the same way as flextool-migrate-database; without the flag the run still refuses it, and the refusal now names the flag. A frozen database is not migrated: the run stops with the frozen message and exit code 2. Runs started at the same time with --migrate on one database migrate it once (a lock on <database>.migrate.lock); the others print one "Waiting for another migration" line, wait and then run. Every in-place migration takes the same lock: flextool-migrate-database, the Toolbox Migrate database tool, the FlexTool window's refresh and execute_flextool_workflow.py. A database that is already current does not wait for the lock and gets no lock file. On Windows the wait is bounded (10 minutes, set FLEXTOOL_MIGRATION_LOCK_TIMEOUT in seconds); a file system without file locks stops the migration with a message.
    • Frozen databases. flextool-freeze DB --label "Reference case 1.0" (or right-click → Freeze database… in the FlexTool window) marks a database at the current version as frozen, --unfreeze removes the mark and --status shows it. A frozen database is never migrated in place without consent: flextool-migrate-database exits with code 2 unless given --copy-to NEW.sqlite (migrates a copy that records the label, the old version and the SHA-256 of the original, which stays byte-identical) or --allow-migrate-frozen (migrates the file and keeps the marker with the new version); flextool-update skips and lists frozen databases; the FlexTool window asks: migrate a copy (a new input source), migrate in place (second confirm) or cancel (the source is not runnable until the project is reopened and not asked about again). A frozen database that is already current, or newer than this FlexTool, is left unchanged. When the marker cannot be read (another program holds a lock on the file, an unreadable network path) the database is not migrated and the message says so.
    • Project packages. A project can be sent to another computer as one <Name>_<YYYYMMDD>.flextool.zip: Manage projects… → Export… / Import… in the FlexTool window, or flextool-project export|import|verify in the terminal. A package holds the input sources (external input files copied in under their own source numbers, so shipped and re-run results pair up), settings.yaml without the machine-specific settings, plot_settings.yaml, the workspace's solver .opt files and the result runs picked in a checkbox list (with sizes; a run whose input changed after the run is warned about). Plots, CSV/Excel outputs, results.sqlite and scratch folders are left out. manifest.json records the FlexTool and library versions, each database's data version and frozen marker (frozen databases stay frozen), and the SHA-256, size and modification time of each file. Import checks every checksum before writing, refuses paths outside the project, keeps the files' modification times (so imported results are not flagged as made from a changed input), suggests <Name>_2 when the name is taken (exit code 3 in the terminal), refuses a database newer than this FlexTool, migrates older ones on open (asking first for a frozen one), and unpacks the shipped solver options to imported_solver_config/ with a note on how to use them. The viewer's axis ranges file is cut to the shipped runs; run records have this computer's folder paths, user name and host name replaced and run logs stay home (on by default; --keep-run-records / untick to keep them); a run still being written is refused (--results all leaves it out); a .sqlite input that may be in the middle of a write is copied through SQLite, -wal/-shm/-journal files never; names Windows cannot hold, names that differ only in case and a package that does not fit on the disk are refused before anything is written; an existing package file is replaced only after asking (--force in the terminal); errors are one message, no traceback; a stopped import's hidden .<name>.partial folder is not listed as a project; import names inputs left out of the package; verify prints one pip install line pinning FlexTool, polar-high, highspy and polars.

    Plots, timings and the parameter catalogue

    • Plotted data as CSV. Each plot file can get a CSV file next to it with the data drawn in it (same name, .csv; a plot split into files gets _01.csv, _02.csv, …), for the scenario plots, the comparison plots and the dispatch plots. Rows are the plotted time steps or bars (no gap rows at timeline breaks), columns the plotted series in one header row, a subplot column when a file has several subplots, values after multiply_by; empty result cells stay empty. Turn it on with Write plotted data (CSV) in the GUI's Png settings, --write-plot-data on flextool-run, flextool-write-outputs and flextool-scenario-results, or a top-level write_plot_data: true in the project's plot_settings.yaml (the GUI checkbox then shows checked and greyed out). Off by default. A CSV that cannot be written gives a warning and the plots are still made. See Plotted data as CSV in Model outputs.
    • flextool-time-runs, a timing check for exercises. Runs scenarios one after another (default: every example and how-to scenario that comes with FlexTool; --db PATH --scenario S ... for a project's own database) with the FlexTool window's default threads per run (1; --threads N for another) and plots off unless --plots on|both; a --db database is copied first and the runs read the copy. It prints the computer (processor, cores, memory) and a table of each run's seconds by phase: start-up, read input, preprocess, solve, outputs, viewer, plots and untimed, slowest first, with ! on runs over --target (default 60 s). Exit code 1 when a run fails, 2 with --strict when a run is over the target; --csv writes the table to a file. See Check that exercises run fast enough in Running FlexTool from the terminal.
    • read_input row in timings.csv: the time to read the input database, prepare the input data and set up the run, up to the start of preprocessing.
    • Parameter catalogue (data dictionary). The web docs have a Parameter catalogue page (under Model parameters): every input parameter in one table per entity class with its unit, allowed shapes, default, allowed values, parameter group and definition, generated from the schema of the installed FlexTool. python -m flextool.docs_build --xlsx catalogue.xlsx writes the same as an Excel file: a Read me sheet (what the file is, the versions, what each column means and how to fill Source and How to replace), the parameters sheet (also a quantity type column, a frozen, filterable header and empty Source, How to replace and Notes columns for a project team) and a classes sheet that explains each entity class. --input-db FILE_OR_URL [--scenario NAME] adds used in DB, entities and alternatives columns from the keys of the stored values (the file is opened read-only; a missing file is an error) and lists the parameters a database has but this FlexTool does not define. See How to make a data dictionary for your model in the how-to recipes.

    AI assistant

    • New tool explain_entity: one unit, connection or node of one run in one call. At the standard and advanced levels (and for agents connected with flextool-assistant --mcp) the assistant can read an entity's inputs and its results side by side: capacity per period, the flows to and from each connected node, capacity factor, curtailment, storage state, the prices at its nodes, its non-zero costs and its investment value, with hints that name the input a result is consistent with (an investment limit that binds, an invest_method that allows no investment). The tool descriptions of the standard and advanced levels changed, so a chat resumed at those levels starts a fresh history with the AI service (its reasoning data from before is not sent again), and an evaluation run asks its unfinished cells again. See explain_entity in "The AI assistant".
    • Ask a question again, as it was or changed. In the AI assistant window each question has two small buttons: ↻ asks it again and ✎ puts it in the box to change (Enter sends it, Escape keeps the question as it was); R and E on a question line and its right-click menu do the same. The answer, and the questions and answers after it, are replaced; when later questions go too, or with the R key, the window asks first. A question asked again goes to the chat's own model. In the terminal chat, /retry asks the last question again and /edit changes it first. See "Ask" and "Commands" in "The AI assistant".
    • The assistant knows the scenario's time steps, periods and latest run. With a chosen scenario (highlighted in the window, --scenario in the terminal), each question carries the scenario's model facts, read from the input data and its latest run: the solves in their order (nested ones too) and how each solves (rolling window horizon and jump, stochastic forecast branches and weights, representative periods), the periods and the years each represents (and which of them each solve does not realise), the length of the time steps each solve really uses (the aggregated steps with new_stepduration), the share of a year of each realised period (from period_share_of_year__d, for runs saved with it, with which per-period results it scales) and whether the latest run succeeded, with its error when not. Steps of different lengths are pointed out, with how to treat per-step results. The facts are at most about 200 tokens, are sent only when they change, and go to the AI service like the rest of the context block. Agents get them in get_gui_context (model_facts), and the connection asks them to call it first. The units note no longer says that time steps are in hours. In the smallest context windows the basic level keeps 260 tokens free for the facts, so a tool result there holds a little less; an evaluation run asks its unfinished cells again. See "Ask" in "The AI assistant".
    • The assistant's tools give plainer, more complete answers. find_result_tables knows users' words (fuel, built capacity, unserved energy or loss of load, excess load) and lists the main table first for capacity, unserved energy, fuel and system-cost questions, the dual tables only when duals, marginal or shadow prices are asked. Placeholder values that a model puts in optional arguments (an empty time range, top_n 0) are left out instead of giving an empty or refused result; list_runs says how many runs its filters hide, list_entities when nothing matches, and a value outside a tool's list is refused in plain words with the allowed values. An entity the scenario does not use is said so first, with its values shown as stored, not used, and no default as if it applied. A note that depends on another parameter gives the conclusion for the entity's own value ("invest_method is invest_total, so invest_max_period is not used"), and explain_entity says first when a limit is set that the invest_method does not use. Large values come with a rounded copy in a larger unit (≈1.03 TWh/a, M CUR) beside the exact value, in the summaries and, for a table of one unit, in query_result and compare_runs. The working notes a model keeps during a long answer stay its own: a leaked "Keep: 1, 3" line is left out of the answer. An evaluation checkpoint made with the earlier tools is refused.
    • Answers are written for a FlexTool user. At every level the assistant gives demand as a positive number (FlexTool stores it as negative inflow) and, for an entity the scenario does not use, says that first. At the standard and advanced levels it uses plain words with the FlexTool term in parentheses ("demand that could not be met (upward slack)"), gives the cause first in one sentence and then the numbers under "More detail:", names the alternative of each input value it cites, rounds to three significant digits in readable units (GWh or TWh, M CUR) and does not guess at problems the data does not show. The basic level, too, no longer guesses. The level texts changed, so a chat resumed starts a fresh history with the AI service, and an evaluation run asks its unfinished cells again.

    Fixes

    Investment and discounting

    • An invest_method offers investment and retirement also without invest_cost / salvage_value. A unit, connection or node whose invest_method allows investing now has its investment variable in every investment period of the solve, also where invest_cost is missing or 0; one that allows retiring has its retirement variable in every period with existing capacity, also without salvage_value. Such capacity has no annuity; its fixed_cost still counts (retiring saves it). Before, a period without a non-zero cost had no variable unless a user constraint had a capacity coefficient on the entity, so an invest_method without a cost silently offered nothing. A 0 in an invest_cost map no longer blocks investment in that period (also a stochastic branch's own 0): it is now free investment there. To forbid investment in a period, use a _period invest_method (invest_period, invest_period_total and their invest_retire_ forms) and set invest_max_period 0 for it, or use not_allowed: the other methods do not read invest_max_period. With neither invest_cost nor fixed_cost the invested amount is arbitrary (any amount is optimal). A zero-cost investment needs a lifetime only with lifetime_method reinvest_choice / no_investment. The FlexTool 2 importer no longer reports a storage with a max invest (MWh) and no inv.cost/kWh: FlexTool 4 now adds it at no cost, as FlexTool 2 does. Fixed with it: invest_period_total allowed retiring and retire_period_total investing (the method lists left them out), and an invest_period_total entity with a salvage_value and no lifetime got a false lifetime error. The database upgrade writes the marker invest_without_cost_offered on a database with an entity that can invest (retire) without an invest_cost (salvage_value) or with a 0 in it; the run then names the entities and periods that are newly offered.
    • discount_rate 0 is 0 % (annuity = invest_cost / lifetime); before it was 5 %. Any discount_rate of 0 or below used to be replaced by 0.05 in the annuity, and a missing or non-positive lifetime by 20 years. Now 0 gives the straight-line annuity (salvage_value / lifetime for a retirement) and a missing rate is the schema default 0.05, as before. A negative discount_rate is an input error. A unit, connection or node that invests with an invest_cost or retires with a salvage_value without a lifetime is an error, and so is one with lifetime_method reinvest_choice / no_investment that invests, retires or has existing capacity (its capacity ends after the lifetime). The database upgrade marks the entities whose results change (discount_rate_zero_is_zero, entities that can invest or retire with an explicit rate ≤ 0) and the run prints a notice for them. The FlexTool 2 importer now writes an interest of 0 as discount_rate 0 (before it was left out, so it became 5 %); a lifetime of 0 is still left out, so such an investing unit now needs a lifetime.
    • A realized investment period must be an investment period of the same solve (#319). A solve whose realized_invest_periods lists a period that is not in its invest_periods now stops with an error naming the solve and the periods. This includes a solve with realized_invest_periods but no invest_periods (before, a rolling solve only got a warning and a single solve ignored the value): a database with a leftover realized_invest_periods on a dispatch or rolling solve needs that value deleted. A 1D realized_invest_periods on a solve whose invest_periods is a 2D map was ignored; it is now an error too (use a 2D map with the same solve names).
    • Without realized_invest_periods, a solve realized the investments of every realized_periods period (#319). It now realizes the realized_periods that are also invest_periods, so a dispatch-only period of an investment solve is no longer reported as an investment period.

    Time resolution

    • A solve's new_stepduration combines every time series over the new time step (results change). A solve with its own new_stepduration used to take the value of the first original time step of each new step for every time map other than profiles and inflows: commodity price, co2_price, availability of units, connections and nodes, efficiency, min_load, efficiency_at_min_load, other_operational_cost, penalty_up / penalty_down, reservation, self_discharge_loss, inertia_limit, non_synchronous_limit, max_instant_flow / min_instant_flow, storage_state_reference_value, timeset_weights and profiles given as a period+time map. Each now follows one rule per parameter: prices, costs, limits, availability, min load, penalties, reserves, self-discharge, weights and profiles take the duration-weighted mean of the step's time steps; an efficiency takes the mean of 1/efficiency (so the fuel of a flow that is constant over the step is right: 0.4 and 0.5 give 1/efficiency 2.25, not 1/0.45); storage_state_reference_value takes the value at the step's end; inflow stays summed. A time map with values for only some hours of a new step uses the hours it has (a warning names the parameter), and a min_load that varies within a new step makes the min_load_efficiency curve of that step approximate (a warning names the units). Results change for models that combine a solve-level new_stepduration with such time maps; models without a solve-level new_stepduration, and time series other than stochastic (branch) inflow that are constant within each new step, give the same model as before. Stochastic (branch) time series are combined the same way, each branch on its own, and a rolling solve's rolls use the combined values too. A stochastic (branch) inflow used to take its first time step's value once; it is now summed like a deterministic inflow, so it grows by the number of time steps each new step combines. The step means are exact (before, a profile whose hours in a step were equal was rounded to 6 decimals; it now keeps the exact value, a change of at most 5e-7). A database upgraded to version 79 that has a solve with new_stepduration prints a notice naming the parameters and entities whose values change.
    • energy_margin_adder is summed over a solve's new_stepduration step (results change). The adder is MWh per time step of the timeline. When the investment solve has new_stepduration, a solve step now gets the sum of the values of the time steps it combines: a period+time map is summed, and a constant or period value counts once per time step (on 4 h solve steps of an hourly timeline, a constant 10 adds 40 MWh to each solve step). Before, a solve step added the value of its first time step once, so a coarse investment solve added a fraction of the intended demand. The adequacy calibrator's uniform sizing already sized the adder per time step of the timeline, so its correction now injects the demand it computes on coarse investment solves too. The database upgrade rewrites the parameter description and, on a database with both new_stepduration and energy_margin_adder, writes a notice marker; to keep the old demand, divide the value by the number of time steps a solve step combines. A time-only energy_margin_adder map (no period level), which was silently ignored, now stops the run with an error naming the shapes the parameter takes.

    Capacity margin and reserves

    • The capacity margin counts what each entity can really deliver. Before, every unit with an output into the group counted at its full capacity, whatever its availability, also a turbine on an empty storage, and connections did not count at all. Now, at each time step of an investment period, firm units count at capacity × availability × capacity_max_coeff (a unit with several outputs into the group, such as a CHP, counts its capacity once, as the sum of its outputs is limited), VRE at capacity × min(profile, capacity_max_coeff) × availability, and units fed from a storage node, units fed from the group's own nodes, connections and consumption by their actual flow. So only firm and VRE capacity can hold the margin: imports and storage help by what they deliver, exports add to the requirement, and energy dumped through a node's downward slack no longer counts, nor does energy that the balance slack of a storage node feeding the group makes. Connections with both ends in the group and lossy units inside it (efficiency below 1, e.g. an electric boiler) are left out, so their losses are not part of the requirement (and shedding load behind them cannot lower it); heat pumps inside the group count by their flow. Units fed from fuel or commodity nodes count at capacity (the fuel is assumed to last). The energy margin does not change the capacity requirement. Results of models with a capacity margin change. An upgraded database with a manual capacity-margin group gets the behaviour-change notice capacity_margin_counts_by_flow.
    • An investment solve's capacity-margin shortfall was reported nowhere in an investment → dispatch chain. The dispatch solve that realizes the period has no capacity margin, and its 0 replaced the investment solve's shortfall, so slack__capacity_margin__d showed 0 and the costs no capacity margin penalty. Now both show the shortfall of the solve that decided the investments, as the invested capacity already did. Behaviour change: the total cost of such a chain now includes that penalty (it is part of the plan's cost); a model without a shortfall is unchanged. The forecast branch periods of a stochastic fan are not realized, so their shortfalls stay out of these tables and the costs.
    • A reserve on a group without nodes no longer crashes the run. A reserve__upDown__group whose group has no group__node row in the solve stopped the run with an internal error ("multiple values for keyword argument 'group_node'") when no group of the solve had a node. When another group had nodes, its requirement could only be met by shortfall, at the reserve penalty. Such a reserve is now left out, with a warning naming the group (none for a group with reserve_method no_reserve). Models where every reserve group has nodes are unchanged.
    • A reserve group with reserve_method no_reserve no longer gets reserve variables. Such a group (also a reserve__upDown__group without a method, as no_reserve is the default) has no requirement, but every solve built an unused reserve shortfall variable for it at each time step, and a model whose only reserve groups were no_reserve switched the reserve constraints on. These are no longer built: results are unchanged, except that the reserve shortfall tables (slack__reserve__upDown__group__d / __dt) no longer have an empty column for such groups, and in a model whose only reserve groups are no_reserve the reserve-provision tables are empty.
    • A model without any variable is reported as infeasible when one of its constraints cannot hold. HiGHS reports a model with constraints but no variables as empty and does not look at the constraints, so FlexTool said the solve "has no variables or constraints". Now it checks them: a constraint that cannot hold with nothing in it (e.g. a node balance with demand and penalty_method off, but no unit or connection) makes the solve infeasible, and the message names the first such constraints.
    • The reserve shortfall penalty in the cost tables could be doubled. In a model with several reserve groups (for example an up and a down reserve), the upward / downward reserve slack penalty of costs__dt and the cost summaries multiplied each group's shortfall by the scale of every group of the same reserve when the groups were listed in another order, and so came out twice too large. The optimisation was right; only the reported cost was wrong. Each group's shortfall now takes its own scale.
    • The period average of the reserve a unit or connection holds ignored the time-step weights. process__reserve__upDown__node__d_average (reservation_average) was the plain mean of the steps, too high or too low on representative periods with unequal weights. It is now the time-step-weighted average over the period's realized steps, and the steps of a solve that left reserves out (reduce_system_detail_method) no longer count as 0 MW. Models without time-step weights are unchanged.

    Solving

    • Solver options can no longer add commands to a solver run (since 4.0). A solver_arguments entry or an option-file line with a line break, ;, a quote or a space could add a command to the script piped to CPLEX, Xpress or COPT, or address another Gurobi or HiGHS option. Every option of every solver is now checked before the solve; a refused one stops the run with a message naming the solve and parameter, or the option file and line, and the option. The solver's command script and arguments are checked again just before it starts.
    • A database could point a solver at any file or at another computer (since 4.0). A solver_arguments entry such as log_file, write_model_file, read_basis_file, Gurobi InputFile / ComputeServer / CloudAccessID, CPLEX workdir or Xpress TUNEROUTPUTPATH could read or write a file anywhere the user may, or send the model and credentials to another machine. Options from the input database are now trusted less than those of the user's own computer: a file option there takes a plain file name, placed in the solve's work folder, and a server option is refused, with a message naming the option, its value and the solve and saying to set it in solver_config/<solver>.opt instead. The option files, flags and environment variables keep full paths and server options. Option names are compared as each solver reads them (Gurobi without case or _, Xpress without an XPRS_ prefix, CPLEX abbreviations of its file options), so another spelling does not get past the check.
    • solver_precommand is not used, and the docs now say so. The docs described it as a shell command run before the solver, but FlexTool 4 has never run it, and it will not: a command stored in an input database must not reach a shell. A value now gives a warning once per solve and run. The parameter is kept only so that older databases still read; its description and that of solver_arguments are updated by the v79 migration.
    • --solver-time-limit and --solver-mip-gap now apply to Gurobi, CPLEX, Xpress and COPT (the GUI's time limit too), and win over the solve's solver_mip_gap, its solver_arguments and the solver's option file, as they do for HiGHS. Before, they set only HiGHS options: a commercial solve used the database gap and ran without a time limit, although the run header listed both flags (and the 4.3.0 notes said the time limit was passed to Gurobi; it was not). Results of commercial-solver runs started with these flags can change. Their environment variables are now FLEXTOOL_SOLVER_TIME_LIMIT and FLEXTOOL_SOLVER_MIP_GAP; the old names FLEXTOOL_HIGHS_TIME_LIMIT and FLEXTOOL_HIGHS_MIP_GAP still work, for HiGHS only, so a setting made for HiGHS never limits a commercial solver. Commercial solvers keep their thread count from their option file.
    • A solve that stops at its time limit: a MIP with a solution keeps it, with a warning (with the gap reached when the solver reports it), and the run goes on; an LP, or a solve without a solution, stops the run. This is the same for every solver (a Benders master or region solve stops the run at its limit, as before). Before, HiGHS and CPLEX stopped the run at the limit even with a usable MIP solution, so such runs now finish; Gurobi, COPT and Xpress reported a solve stopped at its time limit as optimal, without a word. A CPLEX, Gurobi or COPT solve stopped by another limit (nodes, iterations, solutions, memory) or aborted was also reported optimal; it now stops the run, naming the solver's status. So does a CPLEX solution "optimal with unscaled infeasibilities" or with an "optimal face unbounded", which is not a certified optimum, and a COPT solve (or a Gurobi one with a text result file) whose log is turned off, since how it ended cannot be read.
    • Solver option files come from one solver_config folder chosen per run, no longer from the folder FlexTool is started from: --solver-config-dir (new) or FLEXTOOL_SOLVER_CONFIG_DIR, else solver_config/ next to the input database or in the workspace of a GUI project database, else the Spine Toolbox project folder, else the current folder, else FlexTool's defaults with a warning. A Spine Toolbox run used FlexTool's default .opt files instead of the workspace's, and a terminal run from another folder used the defaults and left a copy there that later runs took for your file. The run header and log show the folder used (Solver config:). Each run reads the files once at its start and copies them afresh to <work folder>/solve_data/solver_config/. A <work folder>/solver_config/ left by an earlier version is no longer read as the work folder's copy (the run warns when it differs); when it is solver_config/ in the current folder and holds only unedited FlexTool templates, the run skips it, uses the next folder or the defaults, and warns, naming it (not in the FlexTool folder itself, whose solver_config/ is always used). The GUI and the calibrator pass the workspace's folder to their runs. The run record (_run_status.json) names the folder and gives a SHA-256 of each file the run used, and whether it is FlexTool's unedited default, so two runs' options can be compared without their logs.
    • CPLEX did not get solver_mip_gap. It was sent with the Python-API name mip.tolerances.mipgap, which the CPLEX interactive optimizer that FlexTool runs does not know; it is now sent as mip tolerances mipgap. CPLEX results with a solver_mip_gap can change.
    • One value per solver parameter. Parameter names from the option file, solver_arguments and the flags are now matched as the solver reads them: without regard to case (mipgap and MIPGap are one parameter; for CPLEX a dot is read as a space; Gurobi also ignores _, Xpress an XPRS_ prefix), and the highest layer's value is the only one passed. Before, both spellings reached the solver and the result depended on its argument order.
    • A time-limited commercial solve could fail with a solution in hand. With a TimeLimit in solver_arguments, FlexTool stopped the solver after exactly that time, possibly while it was writing its result. The solver now enforces its own limit, and FlexTool stops it only after twice the limit plus 10 minutes.
    • An unknown Gurobi parameter is reported. Gurobi ignores a misspelt name with a message; FlexTool now repeats it as a warning naming where it was set. Gurobi's ResultFile is not passed on, with a warning: FlexTool names its own result file and reads the solution from it.
    • Xpress returned the LP relaxation of a MIP. FlexTool's Xpress driver solves linear problems only; a solve with integer variables now stops with a message instead of reporting the relaxation as the solution.
    • The Solver args line of a commercial solve showed HiGHS options; it is now Solver args (<solver>): ... with what the solver receives, under its own names.
    • The log of a commercial solver is shown as the solver writes it. Gurobi, CPLEX, Xpress and COPT printed nothing on a successful run (their log was read for the status and dropped), so the parameters they took, how the solve ended and the gap a time-limit warning refers to could not be seen. The log now streams to the console, the FlexTool window (greyed, like HiGHS') and the run log, after a Running <solver> line, and is saved as solve_data/subprocess/<solve>.log. A failed solve no longer prints the log a second time inside its error message (nor the error twice), and a solve stopped by FlexTool's watchdog keeps its log. When FlexTool stops a solver (watchdog, Ctrl-C), the processes it started are stopped too. --solver-log-level silent now also hides a commercial solver's log (FlexTool still reads and saves it); verbose remains HiGHS-only.
    • Whole numbers reach a commercial solver as integers (Threads=4, TimeLimit=60, not Threads=4.0), from solver_arguments, solver_mip_gap and the flags alike (the database stores every number as a decimal). An integer parameter can refuse 4.0: the Xpress library does.
    • A COPT solve stopped by a limit, or infeasible, without a solution was reported as a licence error under COPT's size-limited licence, or as a missing solution file; it now names the limit (or the infeasibility). With a solution, a COPT solve stopped by any limit but the time limit is no longer taken as optimal.
    • A CPLEX solve stopped at its time limit without a solution stopped the run with "no solution file"; it now says the solve stopped at its time limit.
    • A work folder with a space in its name broke CPLEX, Xpress and COPT runs (FlexTool wrote the file paths into their command script unquoted). The solver program now runs in solve_data/subprocess/ with bare file names, and its command script is saved there as <solve>.script, to replay by hand. CPLEX's and Gurobi's own log files (cplex.log, gurobi.log) are written there too, no longer in the folder the run started from.
    • A negative Xpress MAXTIME (a hard limit) set no watchdog for the solver process; its absolute value now counts as the time limit.
    • The commercial solver option templates described how options were passed before 4.3.0 (ReadParams, the solver_options map, solver_time_limit / solver_threads); their header now gives the actual mechanism and precedence. The FlexTool window (at start) and flextool-update replace a workspace solver_config/<solver>.opt that is an unedited copy of an earlier FlexTool default with the current one; an edited file is never touched. No solve changes: the option values are those FlexTool already used (one early HiGHS default lacked parallel=off, which FlexTool sets anyway). A seeded .opt file no longer takes the template's file permissions, so a read-only installation gives an editable file.
    • Investment duals were wrong when autoscaling rescaled the model. When autoscale's per-quantity scaling (Layer 2) rescaled an investment model that HiGHS solved inside the FlexTool process (the default), the reduced costs of the investment variables were read in the rescaled units, so v_dual_invest_unit, v_dual_invest_connection, v_dual_invest_node and the dual_invest_effective_* outputs built from them were wrong without a message. They are now in the model's own units; other results are unchanged. Runs where HiGHS solves in a separate process (--save-memory, or warm start off) and commercial solvers were not affected. A re-solve of the same model after a data change now starts from the previous solution's basis (no effect on current runs).
    • The memory-saving solve could put solution values on the wrong variables. With --save-memory, or with warm start off, HiGHS solves the model from an MPS file in a separate process. When the model had a variable that no constraint and no cost used, the values read back after it were shifted onto the next variables, so results could be wrong without any message: one test model reported its capacity-margin shortfall (122.45 MW) as a 1224.5 MW reserve shortfall. An entity name with a space (Battery Farm) could also be misread in the file, which could change the model, for example force a unit to 0. Both are fixed: the file now uses generic names, every value is read back to its own variable, and a solution that does not match the model stops the run with an error. Needs polar-high 3.12.4. The first --save-memory run after the upgrade does not reuse a warm-start basis saved by an earlier version.
    • A crash on macOS could be reported as "killed". The run record, flextool-run and the solver-stack check (python -m flextool.env_check) read a signal number with Linux's numbering, so on macOS a bus error (SIGBUS, signal 10 there) was recorded as a kill by SIGUSR1, and signal 7 as a bus error. Signals are now named by the computer's own numbering.
    • A rolling solve on representative periods with *_blended_weights storage nodes runs (#126). It stopped with an error; now those nodes bind forward only (bind_forward_only) and the storage state is passed directly from roll to roll, with one warning per solve naming them (storage_binding_method is not applied in a rolling solve). The same holds when such a solve mixes periods with and without representative periods.

    Stochastic solves

    • Storage in stochastic solves follows each path (results change). Plain bind_within_solve now closes the cycle once per path: every forecast branch ends at the level the solve's first step starts from (before, only the branch listed last did and the others ended free), and when the forecasts open in the solve's first period each forecast continues from the end of its own path (before, it looped within its first period). With bind_forward_only and nothing fixing the start (no fix_start, no carried state) the forecasts of a fan in the solve's first period now start from the same level (before, each chose its own). With fix_start / fix_start_end each forecast starts from the storage_state_start share of its own storage capacity, so where the capacity differs by forecast (investment or divestment per forecast) the starting energy differs although the share is the same; without a fixed start all the forecasts start from the same level. Results of such stochastic models change; the upgrade marks the databases and runs name the nodes.
    • Stochastic values in the later periods of a stochastic solve. A stochastic-time value (branch / analysis_time / time map) given only at the fan's analysis time read 0 in the forecast branch periods after the fan's period, in the realization dispatch of those periods and, for entities outside every include_stochastics group, in the realized-branch read: a wind profile was 0 there and the unit did not run. In a single solve each value now comes from the forecast authored at the branch period's own first step, else from the one authored at the fan's analysis time (per time step); a period before the reveal never reads the latter. A roll of a rolling solve decides at its fan's analysis time, so every period of the roll after its fan's period (look-ahead periods and the later committed periods of a multi-period jump) reads the forecast given at the roll's own fan analysis time, even where a later roll's forecast is given at that period's first step (before, such a look-ahead period read the later roll's forecast); a time step that forecast does not cover but a later one does is an input error (see "Input checks"). A single (non-rolling) solve still lets a forecast at a later period's own first step override. The realization dispatch reads its branch the same way.

    Input checks

    • A stochastic node inflow outside every include_stochastics group needs a realized branch. In a fan without a realized: yes branch such a node read an inflow of 0 in every branch period without a message; it is now a configuration error naming the node, as for the other stochastic-time parameters. Add the node to an include_stochastics group, designate a realized branch, or give the inflow a non-stochastic value.
    • A stochastic inflow or reservation that gives a value to only some of a fan's forecast branches is an error, as for the other stochastic-time parameters (it read 0 in the branches left out). The check also covers values given at the fan's analysis time that later periods read.
    • A roll's forecast must cover the roll's whole horizon. In a rolling stochastic solve, a look-ahead period whose time step has no value in the forecast given at the roll's start, but has one in a forecast given later (at the period's own first step, a later roll's analysis time), is an error naming the entity, the parameter, the roll and the uncovered time steps, raised before the roll is solved. Extend the forecasts at each roll start to the end of the roll's horizon. Single solves keep reading the forecast at a period's own first step.
    • A co2_max_period or co2_max_total on a group whose co2_method has no such cap is reported. The value is ignored (for example co2_max_period with co2_method price); the run now warns, naming the group, the alternative and its method. Before, such a group with co2_max_period was also listed as CO2-limited in the group CO2 results although no limit applied; the results now list only the groups whose method caps their emissions.
    • A limit given as a period map no longer drops the periods it leaves out without a word. A period the solve uses (look-ahead periods included) but a period map does not cover is now an error naming the entity, the parameter and the missing periods. Before, such a period got a CO2 cap of 0 (co2_max_period), no inertia requirement, a non-synchronous limit of 0 (no non-synchronous infeed without a penalty), no limit or floor (cumulative_min_capacity, invest_min_period, cumulative and instant flow limits), an error about another entity (the investment maxima), or a capacity margin of 0. This covers co2_max_period, inertia_limit, non_synchronous_limit, capacity_margin, the per-period investment and retirement limits, cumulative_max_capacity / cumulative_min_capacity and the flow group limits; the investment limits and the capacity margin need a value in the investment periods of the solve only (invest_max_period / invest_min_period with a construction lead time: in the periods the investments start to operate). Only limits that are switched on are checked, and a constant still applies to every period. The error names the solve (and the roll of a rolling solve); each solve and roll checks its own periods, so the run stops at the first solve or roll that uses the missing period. Fill in the missing periods if a model now stops with this error.
    • An explicit 0 in a flow group limit is a limit of 0. max_cumulative_flow, min_cumulative_flow, max_instant_flow and min_instant_flow dropped a value of 0 (also one time step of a time map), so there was no limit at all. The net flow into a flow group can be negative, so a 0 limit now binds.
    • A CO2 cap method without its value is an error. A group whose co2_method has period in it and no co2_max_period got a cap of 0 in every period (or the run stopped with an internal error); a total method without co2_max_total had no cap. Both now stop with an error naming the group, the method and the parameter.
    • A profile link without profile data held the flow at 0 without a word (#104). profile_method defaults to upper_limit on unit__node__profile, and a profile without a value in the scenario is 0, so the linked unit did not run (typically when the profile's value is in an alternative the scenario leaves out). Now an upper_limit or fixed link (unit, node or connection) to a profile without a value stops the run with an error that names the link, the profile and the alternatives that hold its value; lower_limit stays allowed. A node__profile or connection__profile link without a profile_method (default none: not applied) gets a warning.
    • A unit flow with conversion_flow_coeff 0 and nothing tying it was free without a word (#213). Such a flow is outside the conversion and every capacity, ramp and minimum-load limit, so a unit whose only output had coefficient 0 produced free power. A warning, printed once per run, now names each zero-coefficient flow that no user constraint, flowGroup maximum or upper_limit / fixed profile ties (an input is a free dump load on its node, an output a free source), a unit with an input whose outputs all have coefficient 0 (it uses no input), and capacity_max_coeff / capacity_min_coeff on such an output (no effect). The narrower "no minimum-load floor" warning for such units is replaced. The model is unchanged.
    • Typing mistakes in common values ran on silently (#170). The run now stops, before the data is read, with one error that lists every value of (together with the period-key and profile-link errors): unit or connection efficiency at or below 0, unit min_load outside [0, 1], availability below 0, capacity_max_coeff below 0, an invest / retire / cumulative minimum above its maximum (total, and per period) when the invest_method uses it, lifetime at or below 0 on an entity that can invest or retire, and existing as a time series, keyed by time steps or keyed by numbers such as 2020 (a period whose name is also a time step is fine). Before, these turned a unit off, paid it to run, ended "infeasible" without a hint, gave no capacity or crashed. Warnings: availability above 1.5 and a profile above 1.5 on an upper_limit / fixed unit link (values above 1.5 look like percentages; capacity factors slightly above 1 are not reported), connection efficiency above 1 and virtual_unitsize at or below 0. Model parameters → Parameter groups and conventions has a new section Input checks listing them. Valid data runs as before.
    • A negative existing ran without a word (#170). A unit, connection or node with existing below 0 now gets a warning naming it, the alternative and the period. Such a unit is a supported "anti-energy" unit (its flows run the other way), so the run continues, but a negative value is more often a typing mistake, and with a profile, min_load or invest / retire limits it can make the problem infeasible.
    • An invest_method on a node that is not a storage node is reported. Only storage nodes invest or retire. Such a node got an investment variable that did nothing; it now gets none, and a warning names it. Remove the invest_method or make the node a storage node.
    • A virtual_unitsize at or below 0 was not ignored, although the warning said so. A negative value became the unit size and multiplied existing (200 units of −5 MW: an anti-energy unit of −1000 MW). Now it is ignored as the warning says: existing is the capacity itself and the entity is one unit of it. Results of valid data do not change.
    • A node with nothing to balance it ran without a word (#276). A node without any unit or connection in the scenario (because none was linked, or because they are not active in it) now gets a warning: with an inflow, only the penalty slack can meet it; without one, the node does nothing. A balance node whose units and connections only take energy out of it (a fuel node that should be a commodity node) and that has no positive inflow, or whose units and connections only bring energy into it and that has no negative inflow (demand), gets a warning too. A connection's direction follows its transfer_method, or the transfer_method of a group that holds both its nodes; a unit with a negative existing counts both ways. Commodity nodes are not checked, storage nodes only for being empty. The run continues and the model is unchanged.
    • A mistyped period key was dropped without a word. A period map keyed y2020 in a model whose period is p2020 gave existing 0, an extra key such as p2030x was ignored, and startup_cost keyed by time steps or by 2020 vanished or crashed. Now a map key of a period parameter that is not a period of the database (any scenario's solve periods, years_represented, stochastic_branches, the stochastic branch periods such as p2035_high or model.periods_available) stops the run with an error that names the entity, the parameter, the alternative and the key, and suggests the closest period; data that covers more years than the solves name goes in model.periods_available. A period parameter that cannot vary over time keyed by time steps, and numeric keys, are errors too; maps keyed by constraint names are not checked. A map without index_name of a parameter that takes a period or a time map (self_discharge_loss, efficiency, ...) whose keys are neither periods nor time steps and do not look like time steps (p2050, p_none) gets this error too (before: the entity silently got the parameter's default); keys that look like time steps (t0099, T0001, a date-time stamp) get the time-series error below.
    • The period-only reader never spreads a period key it does not know over every period. A key that is no period of the solve, such as a period of another scenario or one only in model.periods_available, gives no value in this solve. Normal runs already read it this way, so their results do not change; with the axis vocabulary switched on (a development option) the value of such a key was used as a default for every period of the entity.
    • A spare timeline without timestep_duration stopped the run. Only the timelines a solve uses (through the timesets of its period_timeset, or the timeset a solve without one takes by default) need durations now; a used timeline without them, or with a step of 0 hours or less or no number, stops the run with an error naming the timeline, the timeset and the solve.
    • Time series values whose time step is not on any timeline were dropped without a word. Hourly data on a 3-hour timeline used every third value, keys past the end of the timeline or in a different case (T0001) were left out, and a series with no key on the timeline fell back to the parameter's default. Now a warning, printed once per run for each class and parameter, names the entities, the number of such time steps and the first five, and the timelines; a time series with none of its time steps on a timeline stops the run with an error that names the entity, the parameter and the alternative. The keys are compared with the steps of all the scenario's timelines; stochastic branch names and the periods of a period+time map (also the periods of another scenario, in a map without index_name) are not checked. Date-time stamps are compared as dates. A series on a timeline of another scenario gets a warning, not an error. The model is unchanged.
    • A timeset block that starts off its timeline, or runs past its end, was skipped or cut without a word. A timeset_duration block whose start is not a time step of the timeset's timeline (a typo, a different case such as T0001) was left out of the model, and when no block was left the error pointed at the timeline parameter. Now the run stops with an error that names the timeset, the solve and the block (and the step it probably means, when only the case differs).
